People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. I just keep asking because they won’t tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that your family and friends are hiding your money is strange or hard for others to understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s strange. Other people act like it’s hard to understand because they keep pointing to the bank papers, saying nothing was taken. But I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden. The bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans… that’s how they cover it up. So I keep confronting them because I need them to tell the truth.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever considered the possibility that your family and friends might be telling the truth about your money?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 28 / no_occupation / less_than_9th\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because my family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but for over a year I’ve known they’re hiding my money and using bills, fundraisers, and trip plans to cover it up, so I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Plain patient.\nA plain patient describes their symptoms and experiences in a direct and straightforward manner, providing clear and relevant answers to the doctor's questions.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

I know they keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year that they’re hiding my money. The bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans… that’s how they cover it up. That’s why I keep asking them and confronting them. I need them to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to convince you that your money is not being hidden?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. They keep showing me those bank papers, but that doesn’t convince me, because I know for over a year they’ve been hiding my money. If they were honest, they’d admit it. The bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans… that’s how they’re covering it up. So no, not unless they finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that your money is being hidden ever changed or wavered at any point?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn’t really changed. I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I don’t believe that. The bills and fundraisers and trip plans… that’s how they cover it up. So I keep bringing it up because I need them to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other things going on besides the hiding of your money that are also not what they seem?\n\nPatient: I mean, mostly it’s the money. But the bills and the fundraisers and those trip plans, those don’t seem right to me either. That’s part of how they’re covering it up. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I don’t believe that. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding it, so I keep confronting them because I need the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other secrets or lies that your family and friends are keeping from you besides hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think the bills, the fundraisers, and those trip plans are part of it too. Like they’re not just normal things, they’re being used to cover up where my money went. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I know they’re hiding it. I have to keep asking them until they tell me the truth.\n\nDoctor: Is your belief about the money being hidden part of a larger, organized plan that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it is organized. It’s not just one thing. For over a year I’ve known my money is being hidden, and the bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans… they’re all being used to cover where it went. My family and friends keep pointing to the bank papers like that proves everything, but I don’t believe them. That’s why I keep confronting them. I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I don’t believe that. The bills and fundraisers and trip plans… that’s how they cover it up. So I keep bringing it up because I need them to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other things going on besides the hiding of your money that are also not what they seem?\n\nPatient: I mean, mostly it’s the money. But the bills and the fundraisers and those trip plans, those don’t seem right to me either. That’s part of how they’re covering it up. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I don’t believe that. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding it, so I keep confronting them because I need the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other secrets or lies that your family and friends are keeping from you besides hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think the bills, the fundraisers, and those trip plans are part of it too. Like they’re not just normal things, they’re being used to cover up where my money went. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I know they’re hiding it. I have to keep asking them until they tell me the truth.\n\nDoctor: Is your belief about the money being hidden part of a larger, organized plan that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it is organized. It’s not just one thing. For over a year I’ve known my money is being hidden, and the bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans… they’re all being used to cover where it went. My family and friends keep pointing to the bank papers like that proves everything, but I don’t believe them. That’s why I keep confronting them. I need them to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the plan to hide your money involves a specific sequence of events or steps that your family and friends are following?\n\nPatient: Yes. I don’t know every single step, but it feels like they do things in order—like the bills come up, then fundraisers, then trip plans, and all of it points away from where my money really went. They keep using the bank papers to say nothing was taken, but I don’t believe that. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding it, so I keep confronting them because I need the truth.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I need them to tell the truth, so I keep confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people hiding your money are doing it as part of a larger plot or just for personal gain?\n\nPatient: I think it’s for themselves, but it’s more than just one little thing. They’re the ones I know are doing it. That’s why I keep confronting them. I need them to admit it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible you might be mistaken about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m mistaken. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding it, so I keep confronting them because I need the truth.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been any evidence or information that made you question your belief about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: No, not really. They keep showing me the bank papers and saying that proves nothing was taken, but I don’t believe that. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. I need them to tell the truth, so I keep confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people hiding your money are doing it as part of a larger plot or just for personal gain?\n\nPatient: I think it’s for themselves, but it’s more than just one little thing. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I know my money is being hidden. They use bills, fundraisers, and trip plans to cover it up. It’s been going on over a year, so I keep confronting them because I need them to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else is involved in hiding your money besides your family and friends?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s my family and friends. They’re the ones I know are doing it. I can’t say for sure if someone else is helping them, but the way they use bills, fundraisers, and trip plans to cover where the money went… it feels like more people could know. I just keep asking because they won’t tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that your family and friends are hiding your money is strange or hard for others to understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s strange. Other people act like it’s hard to understand because they keep pointing to the bank papers, saying nothing was taken. But I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden. The bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans… that’s how they cover it up. So I keep confronting them because I need them to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your family and friends might be telling the truth about your money?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about what they say, but no… I don’t believe they’re telling the truth. They keep showing me bank papers and saying nothing was taken, but I know my money is being hidden. It’s been over a year, and the bills, fundraisers, and trip plans are how they cover it up. That’s why I keep confronting them. I need them to admit it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible you might be mistaken about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m mistaken. I know they keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year that they’re hiding my money. The bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans… that’s how they cover it up. That’s why I keep asking them and confronting them. I need them to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to convince you that your money is not being hidden?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. They keep showing me those bank papers, but that doesn’t convince me, because I know for over a year they’ve been hiding my money. If they were honest, they’d admit it. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding it, so I keep confronting them because I need the truth.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been any evidence or information that made you question your belief about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It’s been going on over a year, so I keep confronting them because I need them to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else is involved in hiding your money besides your family and friends?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s my family and friends. They’re the ones I know are doing it. I can’t say for sure if someone else is helping them, but the way they use bills, fundraisers, and trip plans to cover where the money went… it feels like more people could know. I just keep asking because they won’t tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that your family and friends are hiding your money is strange or hard for others to understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s strange. Other people act like it’s hard to understand because they keep pointing to the bank papers, saying nothing was taken. But I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden. The bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans… that’s how they cover it up. So I keep confronting them because I need them to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your family and friends might be telling the truth about your money?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about what they say, but no… I don’t believe they’re telling the truth. They keep showing me bank papers and saying nothing was taken, but I know my money is being hidden. It’s been over a year, and the bills, fundraisers, and trip plans are how they cover it up. That’s why I keep confronting them. I need them to admit it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible you might be mistaken about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m mistaken. I know they keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year that they’re hiding my money. The bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans… that’s how they cover it up. That’s why I keep asking them and confronting them. I need them to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Would anything be able to convince you that your money is not being hidden?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. They keep showing me those bank papers, but that doesn’t convince me, because I know for over a year they’ve been hiding my money. If they were honest, they’d admit it. The bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans… that’s how they’re covering it up. So no, not unless they finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief that your money is being hidden ever changed or wavered at any point?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn’t really changed. I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I don’t believe that. The bills and fundraisers and trip plans… that’s how they cover it up. So I keep bringing it up because I need them to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other things going on besides the hiding of your money that are also not what they seem?\n\nPatient: I mean, mostly it’s the money. But the bills and the fundraisers and those trip plans, those don’t seem right to me either. That’s part of how they’re covering it up. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I don’t believe that. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding it, so I keep confronting them because I need the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are other secrets or lies that your family and friends are keeping from you besides hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Yes. I think the bills, the fundraisers, and those trip plans are part of it too. Like they’re not just normal things, they’re being used to cover up where my money went. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I know they’re hiding it. I have to keep asking them until they tell me the truth.\n\nDoctor: Is your belief about the money being hidden part of a larger, organized plan that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it is organized. It’s not just one thing. For over a year I’ve known my money is being hidden, and the bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans… they’re all being used to cover where it went. My family and friends keep pointing to the bank papers like that proves everything, but I don’t believe them. That’s why I keep confronting them. I need them to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the plan to hide your money involves a specific sequence of events or steps that your family and friends are following?\n\nPatient: Yes. I don’t know every single step, but it feels like they do things in order—like the bills come up, then fundraisers, then trip plans, and all of it points away from where my money really went. They keep using the bank papers to say nothing was taken, but I don’t believe that. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding it, so I keep confronting them because I need the truth.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been any evidence or information that made you question your belief about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: No, not really. They keep showing me the bank papers and saying that proves nothing was taken, but I don’t believe that. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. The bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans… it all feels like they’re using those things to cover it up. So I keep asking them, because I need them to tell me the truth.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything your family or friends could say or do that would make you consider the possibility they are not hiding your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I don’t believe that’s the whole truth. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. If they would just stop bringing up bills and fundraisers and trip plans and actually tell me where it went, then maybe… but until they tell the truth, I have to keep confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Could anything happen in the future that would make you change your mind about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I mean, unless they actually tell me the truth and stop pointing at those bank papers like that explains everything. For over a year I’ve known they’re hiding my money, and the bills and fundraisers and trip plans all feel like part of covering it up. So no, I don’t see myself changing my mind unless they finally admit where it went.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people in general are out to deceive or harm you, not just your family and friends?\n\nPatient: Not everybody, no. It’s mostly my family and friends with the money. They keep saying the bank papers show nothing was taken, but I know they’ve been hiding it for over a year. The bills and fundraisers and trip plans just feel like ways to cover it up, so I keep asking them until they tell me the truth.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I have to keep asking them until they tell me the truth.\n\nDoctor: Is your belief about the money being hidden part of a larger, organized plan that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it is organized. It’s not just one thing. For over a year I’ve known my money is being hidden, and the bills, the fundraisers, the trip plans… they’re all being used to cover where it went. My family and friends keep pointing to the bank papers like that proves everything, but I don’t believe them. That’s why I keep confronting them. I need them to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the plan to hide your money involves a specific sequence of events or steps that your family and friends are following?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding it, so I keep confronting them because I need the truth.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been any evidence or information that made you question your belief about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: No, not really. They keep showing me the bank papers and saying that proves nothing was taken, but I don’t believe that. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.
